## OpenClaw runtime bug — oversized OpenAI Responses input ids
- User asked whether recent sessions showed LLM failures: `OpenAI API error (400): Invalid 'input[112].id': string too long. Expected a string with maximum length 64, but got a string with length 383 instead.`
- Investigation found at least two recent main-agent sessions with this exact shape: current/direct session had `input[112].id` length 383; one triage-dispatched main session had `input[39].id` length 334 and repeated the same failure 4 times.
- Root cause hypothesis: OpenClaw transcript/model-adapter is persisting/replaying assistant tool-call content IDs as long strings like `call_<short>|<large base64-ish blob>` (observed around 364–438 chars). OpenAI Responses API accepts max 64 chars for `input[*].id`, so replaying long tool-call IDs breaks later requests.
- Impact: queue dispatch is healthy after poller timeout/bounded-dedupe fix, but tool-heavy sessions can fail once history with long tool-call IDs is replayed.
- Likely fix: normalize/remap/truncate tool-call item IDs before sending history to OpenAI Responses API; preserve internal mapping if required, and sanitize persisted/generated tool-call IDs so replay does not reintroduce oversized values.

## PR #1512 own_pr_self_review — pushed redaction boundary fix at 4c4d9838
- Gate transition: Runtime build `165294147` completed with failures in `Lobster Runtime — Clippy Lint Clippy` and `Lobster Runtime — Unit Tests Test`; aggregate annotations named failing `plex_client` redaction tests including `non_ok_provision_bodies_are_sanitized_before_error_storage` and body-preview secret redaction cases.
- Source cause: `find_next_query_key` treated any substring from a param-start marker to the next `=` as a key, even if it spanned whitespace/prose (e.g. `Bearer bearer-secret-token-value token=json-secret`). That made `redact_query_key` skip over the later real `token=` key and leak the secret.
- Pushed commit `4c4d9838b5c42199e301fa6d713d47d97096abb6` (`fix(runtime): preserve secret query redaction boundaries`) to require plausible query-key bytes before accepting a query parameter boundary.
- Validation: `./cargo.sh fmt --manifest-path src/lobster_runtime/Cargo.toml --check` passed; `git diff --check` passed; standalone Rust redaction harness covering the failing shapes passed. Repo-local clippy/test remains blocked by private `ecsclient` dependency resolution, so CI annotations plus focused harness/static checks were used as receipts.
- Freshness after push: clean merge-tree against `origin/main@e83941b948571e0fa252f5bcffa0105e973faee1`; GitHub reports `mergeable=MERGEABLE`, `mergeStateStatus=BLOCKED`. After push, only known `GitOps/GitHubPop` failure reported so far; fresh Runtime gates pending/not yet reported.
- Posted PR comment `160923571` and Telegram digest message `3934`.

## CodeClaw own-PR noise policy — tightened Copilot/bot feedback threshold
- Trigger: Eduardo asked whether CodeClaw was over-addressing Copilot comments on own PRs, citing #1681 and #1512 churn. Investigation confirmed some Copilot suggestions were being treated as push-worthy despite being suggestion/maintainability/test-hardening level.
- Changed `AGENTS.md` own-PR duplicate/action guard and `own_pr_comment_response` workflow: non-self human change requests remain actionable, but Copilot/bot feedback now defaults to `no-push` unless it names a concrete correctness/security/compile/test/pipeline failure with a source receipt. Suggestion-only bot comments (`consider`, `could`, doc completeness, extra edge cases, refactors, micro-optimizations, test robustness) must not create commits unless bundled into an already-required blocker fix without broadening validation.
- Goal: reduce CI churn and PR thread noise; use memory/argument resolution for nonblocking bot suggestions instead of commits/PR summaries.
